### 3.31 Sight and GM visibility inside one zone *(captured)*

Added by X04 from P02's second-client capture (`s14`, capture-validation §18) and `s21` (§20.4). One zone process; no other process takes part.

| Event | Sequence (receiver handler) | Docs |
|---|---|---|
| a move brings two players into sight | inside `MapInfo.Sight` (1600 on RouN) the at-move exchanger (`AxialListBriefExchangerAtMove::ali_Work` FUN_004bdde0) sends the mover `0x1C06` + `0x1C19` for the other player and, from the same call, the other `0x1C06` + the mover's last `0x201A` + `0x1C19` 33 ms later; then the mover's `0x201A` / `0x2013` reach the other | Z09 §9.6, Z02 §5.1; capture-validation §18.2 |
| map login next to a player | the player logging in gets `0x1C07` + `0x1C19`, the other `0x1C06` + `0x1C19` 14 ms later (`s21`) | Z09 §9.6, Z01 §6; capture-validation §20.4 |
| a move names an unknown handle | C→Z `0x1C01 NC_BRIEFINFO_INFORM_CMD` (Z FUN_0043e460) → appear packet + last move 3–20 ms later; the requester's `0x1C19` goes to the **other** player (Z02-B3) | Z02 §5.2; capture-validation §18.2 |
| `&hide` | GM `0x242A {288}` (the NOEFFECT form), the others `0x1805` 16 ms later | Z11 §3.1; capture-validation §18.6 |
| `&show` | `0x2428` to both; the others see the GM only after the **GM's** next move (`0x201A` → their `0x1C01` → `0x1C06`; Z11 §3.1's "their next sight exchange" is superseded, §5.6) | Z11 §3.1; capture-validation §18.6 |
| `&dimension n` | `0x1805` only where `l_Main` matches or is −1 (`AxialListPacketBroadcast`, Z01 §3.2); every step of an object within sight of the GM sends an appear and a `0x1C0E` both ways ((original bug) P02-B27) | Z11 §3.1, Z09 §9.6; capture-validation §18.6 |

### 3.32 Skills, stat points, abnormal states and death *(captured)*

Added by X06 from P02's capture batch 4 (`s24`, capture-validation §24–§25; two players A and B in sight). Zone and
Character server only; the log records are in §3.15. The skill book and the reset capsules are item uses (§3.14), the
skill saves part of the zone-change save (§3.13).

| Event | Sequence (receiver handler) | Docs |
|---|---|---|
| empower | C→Z `0x4811 NC_SKILL_EMPOWALLOC_REQ {skill, plus, minus}` (Z `sp_NC_SKILL_EMPOWALLOC_REQ` FUN_0044f150) → D `0x4812` (D FUN_004124c0; 40 bytes, 41 with a partly used scroll; `plus` = `minus` = the new totals; the lock index, the item header and the change info are written only conditionally, the rest are stale bytes ((original bug) P02-B37)) → `0x4813 {0x881}` (Z FUN_0044f7d0) → C `0x4814 {skill, 0x881}` "Skill empower succeeded" (with a scroll also its `0x3001`) in 15–52 ms; the free empower points travel only in `0x900D`, the client counts them itself | Z03 §9.1, Z23 §4, D04 §2; capture-validation §24.3 |
| stat point up | C→Z `0x105C NC_CHAR_STAT_INCPOINT_REQ` (Z FUN_0058ad10) → D `0x10D1 NC_CHAR_FREESTAT_SET_DB_REQ` (D FUN_004135e0; 15 bytes, the whole target array; `nLockIndex` never written, Z07-B2) → `0x10D2 {…, 0xAC1}` (Z FUN_0058be50) → C `0x105F {0}` + `0x1035` (the free-stat bonuses `0x12`…`0x1b`, never the STR total) | Z07 §2.2, Z12 §4.2, D02 §7.2; capture-validation §24.4 |
| stat point down (Stat Reset Scroll) | C→Z `0x1062 NC_CHAR_STAT_DECPOINT_REQ` (Z FUN_0058b3f0) → D `0x10D1` (27 bytes, with `DecItemInfo`) → `0x10D2` → G `0x0459` (logged by the Character server before the zone acts) → C `0x3001` → `0x1065 {0}` + `0x1035`; without a scroll the client sends nothing | Z07 §2.3, D02 §7.2; capture-validation §24.4 |
| cast abort | C→Z `0x2444 NC_BAT_SKILLBASH_CASTABORT_REQ` (Z FUN_0057c480) → 24 ms → `0x2438 NC_BAT_SOMEONESKILLBASH_CASTCUT_CMD` to the sight, caster first → caster `0x2445 {0x20E8}` (the client ignores the code) → `0x243D` 44–60 ms later; no SP cost and no cool-down (set only at the blast) | Z03 §5.4, Z23 §3.1; capture-validation §25.2 |
| ground-target cast (Dash) | C→Z `0x2441 NC_BAT_SKILLBASH_FLD_CAST_REQ` (Z FUN_0057f400) → caster `0x2450 {skill, point, index}` + `0x2435` (6–11 ms), sight `0x2451`; next tick (a moving skill has no arming pass) the blast warps (`smo_WarpTo`, x + 5): a second `0x2450` (index + 1) to the caster and `0x4828 NC_SKILL_WARP_CMD` to all; `0x2457` with the cast's index; `0x2452` 0.36–0.41 s later (the skill's `HitTime`) | Z03 §5.5, Z25 §6.3, Z09 §9.3; capture-validation §25.3 |
| toggle on | C→Z `0x2440 NC_BAT_SKILLBASH_OBJ_CAST_REQ` (Z FUN_0057fa50) → `0x244E` + `0x2435` → next tick `0x245C NC_BAT_TOGGLESKILL_ON_CMD` (caster only), then `0x2457`; the effect (`0x2429` / `0x2427` + `0x1C18`, `0x2452`) 1.32–1.39 s later (`HitTime`); each cast costs `HPRate` × MaxHP (`0x240E`); at low HP `0x2434 {0xFD5}` without `0x244E` ("Failed to cast the skill."); the toggle states' `keepTime` reads 1 385 447 ms ((original bug) P02-B39) | Z23 §2.5, Z03 §5.2, Z22 §10.7; capture-validation §25.4 |
| toggle off | by a cast: `0x245D` + `0x2457` in the blast tick, the states removed at the effect (HP cost again); automatically (weapon unequipped, HP ≤ `TS_HPRATEOFF`): the `0x2428` pairs, then `0x245D`, in the cause's tick; each removed state is broadcast twice (`RemoveSkillAbstates`, then `ase_Routine` FUN_00406910 one tick later, (original bug) P02-B38); a link (`so_LinkTo` → `csl_Toggle_AllOff`) sends only `0x245D`, and the states do not reach the next map | Z23 §2.4; capture-validation §25.4 |
| abnormal-state reset by an action | one `0x2428` in the tick of the cause, as the `AbStateReset` table says; "attack" = an attack start (`0x242B`, a cast), not each swing; "attacked" includes a missed swing | Z09 §28.3; capture-validation §25.5 |
| damage over time | after the set (`0x2429` / `0x2427` + `0x1C18`) the first `0x243C NC_BAT_DOTDAMAGE_CMD` 64–85 ms later, then one per tick period (3.00 s for StaDiaTrumpyPoison, 1.00 s for a toggle's drain, which adds `0x245A` for its area part) to the sight; none at 1 HP (the flat damage is capped at HP − 1, and 0 sends nothing); the state is erased silently at death | Z22 §3.3, §4.2; capture-validation §25.4, §25.7 |
| death by a mob | the killing swing: G `0x042B` + `0xC80A` 6 ms before C `0x104D` → `0x2448 {resthp 0}` → `0x240E {0}`; no `0x2411` where the field's `ExpLostAtDeadByMob` is 0 | Z03 §7.2; capture-validation §9.5, §25.8 |
| revive by skill | at the caster's effect `so_ply_Revival` FUN_0057ab90 sends the dead player `0x4829 NC_SKILL_SOMEONEREVAVALTOME_CMD`, and `0x2452 {targetnum 0}` goes to both (why the added slot is missing is not traced, §5.5); accept C→Z `0x482A NC_SKILL_REPLYREVIVE_CMD` (Z FUN_00418a30) → 4 ms → `0x481E NC_SKILL_REVIVE_CMD {hp, sp, lp}`, StaImmortal `0x242A`, `0x203E`, `0x9010`, the saved states, sight `0x481F` (first field = the **revived** player); the town revive is §3.13 | Z03 §7.3; capture-validation §25.8 |

## 4. Glossary

The vocabulary the documents share, then where function and variable names come from and how the older (pre-PDB)
names map to the official ones. Each term names the place that defines it; read that place for details.

### 4.1 Processes, links and sessions

| Term | Meaning | Docs |
|---|---|---|
| world | one WM with its zones, Character and GameLog server; Login, Account and AccountLog serve every world | §2.1, K01 §1 |
| DataServer | Account, AccountLog, Character and GameLog: one server framework with a different parser class and SQL wrappers each | D01 §1, D05 §7 |
| GameDB | the zone's (and WM's) name for the Character server link (`sock2gameDB`, our `g_GameDBSessions`) | Z01 §1, Z05 §1 |
| ServerInfo, server type | `ServerInfo.txt`: every process with its type (0 Account … 8 OPTool), world, zone number, address and ports, read by `ReadDefineScriptor` (our `LoadServerInfo`) | §2.1, §2.2, K01 |
| OPTool | operator tool (ServerInfo type 8, not shipped) that connects to Login, WM, zones and DataServers | §2.1, W04 §4, Z11 |
| session, handle | one connection object; its `u16` handle is the slot index plus k × pool size and changes on every reuse (Login) | L01 §2.3, W05 §4 |
| handshake | 0x0801 `S2SCONNECTION_RDY` / 0x0802 / 0x0803 between servers; `CheckConnectionValidation` (our `*_CheckAlive`) guards every other handler | §2.3 |
| heartbeat | 0x0804 / 0x0805 after 30 s of silence, close after 180 s more | §2.3 |
| seed, XOR-499 | 0x0807 seed after accept; client → server bytes are XORed with a 499-byte table from that position | §2.3, `docs/protocol/README.md` §1 |
| `NETPACKETZONEHEADER`, ref | the 6-byte `{u16 clienthandle, u32 charregistnumber}` a zone puts before a request about a player and the answer echoes; called *ref* in older docs | §2.3, Z01 knowledge, W01 knowledge |
| zone ring, RNG | each zone of a world connects to the next one (`ZoneConnectSession`) and accepts the previous one (listen type 6); `*_RNG` packets travel round it to find a player or map in any zone; `ZoneRingPacket*` classes build them (`zrpb_Request`, answers `zrpb_Success` / `zrpb_Failure`) | Z01 §10, Z14 §2 |
| validate key, pending login | Login's 32-word world-select key; the WM stores the 0x0C0D in a 0x800-slot table keyed by the key's XOR and takes it once on 0x0C0F | §3.5, §3.6, L01 §2.5 |
| one-time token (OTP) | the MD5 token (`COTPLogin`) with which a client returns from the WM to the world list without the password | §3.4, L01 knowledge |
| link (map change) | moving a player to another map: *link same* (same zone, 0x1809) or *link other* (other zone, reserve 0x180D / 0x180E, then 0x180A to the client); not to be confused with a server link | §3.11, Z01 §7 |
| map-login ticket, pending link | the WM's record of a reserved map change that the next 0x1801 map login at the destination zone consumes | §3.9, §3.11, W01 §7 |
| link end | the WM's `0x180C` / `LinkEndProcessing` after the client's 0x1803: features re-attach the player (KQ, party, guild) | §3.10 |
| start script, certification | the Lua script a zone downloads at start (127.0.0.1:58492, then `cert1.fo.gamigo:80`) that runs the data-load and service stages; the certification thread answers its periodic challenge | Z10 §1–§7 |

### 4.2 Messages and records

| Term | Meaning | Docs |
|---|---|---|
| opcode, dept, cmd | `opcode = dept << 10 \| cmd`; official names `NC_<DEPT>_<NAME>` from the `PROTOCOL_COMMAND_<DEPT>` enums | `docs/protocol/README.md`, §1.1 |
| `REQ` / `ACK` / `CMD` | request / its answer / one-way notification (naming convention of the official names) *(inferred)* | `opcodes-*.md` |
| `PROTO_NC_*` | the packet body struct of an opcode in the PDBs (without the session or ref prefix) | C02 knowledge §3, S01 §2 |
| CHARSAVE, 2WLDMAN | dept 16: the zone's character saves to the Character server (`ws_save2DB`, 0x4001 …) and the `NC_CHARSAVE_2WLDMAN_*` blobs to the WM's cache (`ws_save2worldmanager`) | §2.5, §3.13 |
| blob, WM cache | the character data packets the WM keeps per session from character select on and forwards to each zone at map login | §2.5, §3.8, §3.13, W01 §6 |
| ITEMDB | dept of the zone ↔ Character item transactions; *style 1* reserves cells under a lock index and applies on the answer, *style 2* applies first and disconnects on failure | §3.14, Z05 §1, Z05 knowledge §1 |
| lock index | the per-player 16-bit index of an `InventoryCellLockList` reservation, carried by the DB request and its answer | Z05 §1 |
| NC_LOG, NC_SYSLOG | GameLog records (write-only; only 0x0402 is answered) and the hourly text log | §3.15, D05 |
| error code, result code | the `u16` codes in ACKs; texts and client behaviour in `docs/protocol/client-error-codes.json` | C01 knowledge |
| disconnect code | the reason a server passes when it closes a session (`so_Disconnect(0x684)` …), listed per process | Z01 §8, W05 |

### 4.3 Game objects and data

| Term | Meaning | Docs |
|---|---|---|
| `ShineObject` classes | zone object hierarchy `ShineObject` → `ShineMobileObject` → `ShinePlayer`, `ShineMob`, `ShineNPC`, …; behaviour through vtable slots (`vt 0x664` = byte offset 0x664) | Z01 §4, `rtti.py` |
| object type | `so_ObjectType` (vt 0x4d0): player 2, mini-house 3, NPC 4, mob 5, magic field 6, bandit 8, servant 10, mover 11, pet 12 | Z09 knowledge, Z13 §1, Z10 §6 |
| object handle | the zone's `u16` handle of a map object | Z01 §4 |
| charNo, chrregnum, charregistnumber | the `u32` character number (`tCharacter.nCharNo`); userNo = the account number | D02, §2.3 |
| item key, regnum | the `u64` `SHINE_ITEM_REGISTNUMBER` of an item (`iti_mkregnum`, our `ItemKey_Make`) | Z04 §1.2, D03 |
| inven, cell | `u16 inven = type << 10 \| slot` (`ITEM_INVEN`): box type (9 inventory, 8 equipment, 6 account storage, 4 guild storage, …) and slot | Z04 §1.1 |
| lot | the stack count of a stackable item | Z04 §1.2, Z05 |
| belong | item binding (`ItemBelongAttribute`, `CItemAuthorityBase`): whether an item may be traded, moved, dropped | Z04 §1, Z06 |
| charged item | premium (item-mall) item; charged buffs; the item-shop rows in `Account.dbo.tChargeItem` | §3.26, Z04 §8, W04 §11, Z24 §6 |
| abstate | abnormal state (buff, debuff, status): `AbnormalState*` classes, `AbState.shn`, `AbStateSaveTypeInfo.shn`; the *shelters* `g_AbstateNeverShelter` (Z03's `g_ShelterDie`: states kept while dead) and `g_AbstateShelter` (Z03's `g_ShelterItem`) are restored on revive | Z03 §7.2, Z12, Z21 §5, Z22 |
| stat matrix | `Parameter::Container`, the layered stat table recomputed by `so_allparametercalculate` | Z12 §1–§4 |
| SHN, 9Data | the encrypted data tables in `9Data/Shine/*.shn` (server) and `ressystem/*.shn` (client) | `shn.py`, S01 §6 |
| field, map index, server group | a map and its index; `g_ServerGroup` gives the zone number that hosts a map (99 = this zone, −1 unknown) | Z01 §7, Z01 knowledge |
| sub-layer | a visibility layer of a map; `g_SubLayerCanSee` (6 × 6) says who sees whom | Z02 knowledge, Z18 knowledge |
| instance dungeon, ID, indun, map cluster | per-party / per-raid copies of a map (`MapClusterManager`, categories party / raid); *indun* (Korean 인던) in official names | §3.24, Z08 §4–§5, Z14 §1 |
| server menu | a server-side choice dialog (`ServerMenuActor`, 10 slots) with an NPC-range check (`CurrentMenu::cm_MenuAction`) | Z02 §6.5–§6.6 |
| `RemoveWhen` | why a player leaves a map: `REMOVE_NOW` 0, `REMOVE_LOGOUT` 1, `REMOVE_WARP` 2, `REMOVE_REVIVE` 3; every case except `REMOVE_WARP` saves | §3.12, §3.13, Z16 knowledge |
| mover, minimon, pet | mount (`ShineMover`, object type 11), mini-monster accessory, pet (`ShinePet`) | Z13 §5–§9 |
| Pine, scenario Lua | the map-script language (films, scenario books) and the AI / scenario Lua; separate from the start-script Lua | Z09 §14–§27, Z10 |
| `lsf_Factor` | `g_LUA_SCRIPT_SECURITYFactor`, the hit / damage factor that grows × 1.01 per world minute and that the start script resets to 1.0 (Z03's `g_HitRateScale`) | §5.5, Z10 §6 |

### 4.4 Features

| Term | Meaning | Docs |
|---|---|---|
| raid | expedition: up to 20 members as 4 parties × 5 under one leader | §3.18, W02 §1 |
| guild, academy | guild (WM-owned, zone mirrors) and its guild academy: a second member list of a guild with its own chat, ranks and reward storage | §3.19, §3.20, W03, Z14 §4 |
| guild war, guild tournament (GT) | declared war between guilds; the scheduled tournament with its own map and phases | §3.19, §3.21, W03, Z16 §4–§6 |
| KQ | kingdom quest: a scheduled world event with a join list and its own map | §3.23, W04 §6, Z16 §1–§3 |
| holy promise | the master / apprentice relation (official `CHolyPromise*`, our `HP_*`) | §3.29, W04 §12 |
| mini-house (MH) | the player's private house map object with furniture | §3.29, Z13 §1–§3 |
| wedding | proposal, ceremony in the wedding hall, married state, partner summon | §3.29, Z13 §4 |
| booth | a player's street stall | §3.25, Z06 |
| gamble house | the casino map: coins, dice and slot machines | Z06, W04 §15 |
| prison | a timed jail map for punished players, timer kept by the WM | §3.27, W04 §9 |
| titles | character titles (`CCharacterTitleZone`, dept 24 CT) | W04 §7, Z12 §7 |

### 4.5 Reading the code references

- `@ FUN_xxxxxxxx` / `DAT_…` / `LAB_…`: an address in that process's **2016** binary (`re-data/exports/<bin>`, the same
  addresses in `<bin>-pdb`); `client FUN_…` = the 2016 client. `p+0x…` = a `ShinePlayer` member offset
  (`docs/knowledge/S01-player-offsets.tsv` names them); `vt 0x…` = a vtable slot as a byte offset.
- **Folded functions.** The linker merged identical bodies, so one address can carry several official names: an empty
  `ret 0xc` at FUN_004c2450 is `CQuest::Occure_LevelChange` and what Z01 called `ShinePlayer_HeartbeatNoOp`; the empty
  destructor FUN_0040a550 (Login) is `COTPData::~COTPData` and `~CSendPacket` (our `Pkt_Free`). A name at such an
  address says nothing about the caller's intent.
- **Profiler strings** (`"Class::Method"` labels passed to the profiler) gave most pre-PDB names. Some were
  copy-pasted in the original source (C02-B1, Z10-B20), so a handler can carry another handler's label; §4.7 lists
  the names this misled.
- `_DebugMessageNew` (our `Log`) and `_DMVC` (our `LogLevel`, a line with a level byte and prefix) write the debug log;
  the documents skip them unless the text tells something.

### 4.6 Name sources and the names files

- Order of preference (AGENTS.md): the 2016 server PDBs (*pdb2016*), strings in the binaries, the 2017 client PDB
  (*pdb2017*), the client texts; names marked *(ours)* were made up by an item.
- `tools/protocol-re/names/<bin>/*.json` are merged in file-name order and the later file wins. `S01.json` holds the
  official names only for addresses that no item had named, so where an item named an address before S01, the
  generated docs (`re-data/exports/<bin>` + names) and that item's text keep the item's name. The official name of
  every such address is in `docs/knowledge/S01-renames.tsv` (4253 rows: login 40, account 57, accountlog 54, WM 851,
  zone 3251) and in the `<bin>-pdb` exports.
- Items started after S01 (Z09–Z17, W05 and the later ones) use the official names; L00, W01–W04, Z01–Z08, D01–D05
  and C01 use their own. §4.7 maps the older names that occur at least five times in the documents; for any other one, look the
  address up in `S01-renames.tsv`.
- Names that two items gave the same address (merge conflicts) are cosmetic except the ones in §4.7. In the zone the
  set is mostly Z01's pre-PDB names that Z09–Z15 replaced (219 addresses); Login has 9 (`00-base` vs L01), GameLog 9
  (D05 vs Z19), WM 1.

## 5. Superseded statements

Finished items are not edited. When a later item finds an earlier statement wrong, it records the correction in its
knowledge file (sections named "Corrections", in `docs/knowledge/*.md`), in its own pseudocode, or in P02's capture
tables. This section collects all of them. Read it before you trust an older file at the place listed. Columns: *Older
place* = file and § holding the old text; *Superseded statement* = what that file says (shortened); *Current
statement* = what the 2016 code (or a capture) shows; *Source* = the item and § with the evidence. A row marked
"(addition)" fills a gap the older file left open and does not contradict it. Statements that were only renamed
(S01's official names) are in §4 and `docs/knowledge/S01-renames.tsv`, not here. Corrections that change a bug
entry are also marked in the register (§6.1) and listed in §5.9.

Sources collected: knowledge `L01`, `W01`, `W03`, `W05`, `Z02`, `Z07`, `Z08`, `Z09`, `Z10`, `Z12`, `Z13`, `Z15`, `Z16`,
`Z17`, `Z18`, `Z21`, `Z22`, `Z24`, `Z25`, `Z29`, `D02`, `D06`, `S01` §3.3, `S02`, `C01` §3, `W04` ("Answers to other
items' open questions", part 7), `Z10` (combat factor, part 7), `D05` ("For D01", part 8), `P02` (with
`docs/protocol/capture-validation.md` §11 / §13), and the correcting passages in `W05-process.md` §5.1 / §5.3,
`Z29-exp-tables.md` §2.6, `Z12-stats.md` §1.3, `Z25-skill-specials.md`, `Z09-world.md`, `D06-db-inferred.md`. X01 adds
W01-B5 (§5.3), the Z01 §7.1 and Z12 §7.2 rows (§5.4, §5.5, from re-reading the save paths) and the
Z03 `g_HitRateScale` row (§5.5; Z10 recorded it outside a Corrections section). The part-8 sweep re-read every knowledge heading named "Answers to …" / "For <item>" (D02, D03, D04, D05, Z03, Z07, Z10): they answer open points and add facts; the only contradiction among them not yet listed was D05's 0x2802 entry order (§5.7).

**Added by X03** (after X02's confidence review): every row of `docs/knowledge/X02.md` §0, appended at the end of
the subsection of the older place, with *Source* `X02 §0 (<IDs>)`; the IDs (R1, M14, W3, Q23, …) are the re-check
rows of [`docs/review/confidence.md`](../review/confidence.md) §3, which hold the evidence. A row that refines an X01
row of the same place says so ("extends the S01 row above"). Then every row of the correction sections of X02's
follow-up items, appended the same way: `docs/knowledge/Z31.md` §0 (*Source* `Z31 §0 (Z31 §n)`), `Z32.md`
"Corrections to done items" (`Z32 knowledge "Corrections" (Z32 §n)`), `W06.md` §0 (`W06 §0 (W06 §n)`) and `D08.md` §0
(`D08 §0 (D08 §n)`); the § in brackets is the section of the item's own pseudocode holding the evidence. The Z31 and
Z32 rows on `nrb_ItemBuyGBCoin` are one row (§5.6).

**Added by X04** (P02's capture batches 2 and 3): every row of `docs/protocol/capture-validation.md` §13 whose status
names X01 or X04 and that this section did not hold yet, appended at the end of the subsection of the older place, with
*Source* `P02; capture-validation §n` (the § holding the capture evidence; the `@ FUN_…` come from P02's rows in
`docs/knowledge/P02.md` "Corrections"). Rows the older text already states, rows fixed by an annotation override that
no reader of the owner's text would trip on, and rows for X05 are left out; the list with reasons is in

**Added by X06** (P02's capture batch 4: `s37b`, `s38`, `s24`; capture-validation §22–§25): every row of

### 5.4 Zone core and actions (Z01, Z02)

| Older place | Superseded statement | Current statement | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Z01 (timers) | `g_Now` counts milliseconds; "next save = now + 6000" | `g_Now` (DAT_14d41a70) counts tenths of a second (`zs_mainthreadfunction` FUN_005aca30: `elapsed_ms * 10 / 1000`); `g_Now + N * 10` = N seconds, the save interval is 600 s | Z02 knowledge §1 |
| Z01 §2.1 / Z10 §1 | `zs_start` = FUN_005a9d90, "retry once", "wait while server.running" | FUN_005a9d90 is `zs_ServiceThreadFunction` (`zs_start` is FUN_005ab520); one attempt per host; it polls `zs_mainthread.activ`, then sets `zs_mainthread.stop` | Z10 §1, §2.2, §6.6 |
| Z01 §2.3 loader row `World/ChrCommon.txt` | loaded by `CharClassManager_ccm_Load` | loaded by `ShineCommonParameter::scp_Load` FUN_004c9be0; `ccm_Load` FUN_0044a5c0 runs right after it in the same stage | Z10 §5.4, §7.1 |
| Z01 loader table | file names only | the text-table format (`#table` / `#columntype` / `#columnname` / `#record` …) is `OptionReader` (addition) | Z09 §28.6 |
| Z01 §5.1 0x0806 | the seed is the answer to 0x0806 | sent at accept (§5.1 above); the 0x0806 handler uses its own counter `newseed` and is unreachable in practice (Z18-B2) | Z18 §1.3 |
| Z01 §6 `sp_ConnectFromWhere` | asks the WM (0x082A → 0x082B) where the player connects from | FUN_0043f1f0 sends nothing to the WM: for `adminlevel ≠ 0` it sends the player a 0x2011 notice `"From a.b.c.d"` (login IP `sp_LoginIP` +0x2a7d0), then clears `sp_ConnectFrom` (+0x2a7d4) *(captured)* | Z18 §1.1; P02 |
| Z01 §6 burst | "expired items (`sp_ItemInvenDelete`)" | `sp_ItemInvenDelete(1)` FUN_0051c590 deletes every unlocked inventory item whose `ItemInvenDel.shn` `DelCondition` is `DCT_LINKIN_ALL` (19 event items) at every map login; not expiry | Z18 §4.4 |

The log line is only `sp_NC_MAP_LOGIN_REQ : DataFail [CharID=%s]`: FUN_00452590 looks up the table name and both hashes into the `namestring` ring, but the format uses only the CharID. The MD5 is taken over the **decrypted** buffer (`Encription` in place before `CMD5Checksum::GetMD5(m_pHead, m_nBFSize)`). A table whose length changed never gets there: the SHN key starts from the data length, the header decrypts to garbage, `CDataReader::Read` @ FUN_0062a780 trusts `nNumOfRecord`, `operator new[]` throws `std::bad_alloc` and nothing catches it: the zone crashes at start (`zs_start_sleep` @ FUN_005b67c0, two minidumps; (original bug) P02-B43). Which tables are hashed: the X02 row above *(captured)* | P02; capture-validation §28.6 |

### 5.5 Zone combat, stats and abstates (Z03, Z12, Z22, Z23)

Captured "Mob : 5989" for MobInfo 34 and for another mob *(captured)* | P02; capture-validation §27.6 |

The kick works: `0x0C1A` with the player's real user number and WM handle, the WM's handler @ FUN_00428a40 sends `0x0C17 {0x68B}` (W01 §5.2), `so_banning` saves with StoreType 4 (Z01 §8.3). The cross-zone case behaves as Z11-B6 says (§28.5) *(captured)* | P02; capture-validation §28.4, §28.5 |

## 6. Original-server bugs

Every behaviour of the 2016 servers that looks unintended, so a rebuild can decide per entry to reproduce it (faithful
rebuild) or fix it (deliberate change). One row per bug, keeping the owning item's ID `<item>-B<n>`; the owning item's
document holds the full description where the behaviour is tagged `(original bug) <ID>`.

**Sources.** §6.1 is generated by `docs/plan/status/X06-register.py write` (X06's wrapper, which runs X04's
`docs/plan/status/X04-register.py`, which runs X03's wrapper `docs/plan/status/X03-register.py` around X01's
`docs/plan/status/X01-register.py`; run X06's script, not the older ones, which lack the later sources and marks) from every table row whose first cell is a
`<item>-B<n>` ID in `docs/pseudocode/**/*.md`, `docs/knowledge/*.md`, P02's table in
`docs/protocol/capture-validation.md` §12 and X02's table in `docs/review/confidence.md` §5 (X02-B1 … B12, group
6.1.12; their Evidence column ends with the reachability of `docs/knowledge/X02.md` §3: *latent* = no state the 2016
client produces reaches it). Re-run it after items change; do not edit §6.1 by hand. The wrapper also adds group 6.1.8 for
D08's bugs of the DB servers' common process layer. IDs that
capture-validation lists as "shown on the wire" (§12, second table) get *(captured)* with every § of the capture,
linked to the first; P02's own rows (group 6.1.11) carry *(captured)* with the § where the capture shows them, except
P02-B4 (latent), P02-B26 (masked) and P02-B32 (read in the batch-3 review); P02-B33 … B40 (capture batch 4, X06) cite
capture-validation §22–§25, and P02-B35 joins its extensions (§23.3 kicks inside the logout window, §25.9 the quit
that saves once). "linked: …" at the end of a Handler cell
names an entry of another item that describes the same defect or the same packet (X04). §6.2 is hand-written:
bugs X01 found in free-text notes, client-side mismatches and client text typos. The row of the owning item's own file wins; other files that repeat the ID are named as "also"
(Z19 repeats S02-B11…B21; S01 §1.4 repeats W04-B2/B3/B22/B29 and S01-B1…B3 and is skipped, except its un-numbered
Z04 row on the 0x302F academy reward withdraw, which is an open check, not a confirmed bug: §7.3, Z04 row). The verdict tables in
`knowledge/Z09.md` and `knowledge/Z13.md` reuse bug IDs but are not bug rows and are skipped. Entries a later item
changed are marked **Superseded**, **Narrowed**, **Extended** or **Withdrawn** with the change and a link to §5.9.

**Columns.** *Handler*: the owning row's handler cell (opcode, function, `@ FUN_…`, § of the owning document) plus a
link to the owning document's bug section. *Evidence*: `code` = read in the 2016 code; `code; effect *(inferred)*` =
the behaviour is read in the code, its consequence deduced; *(inferred)* = the behaviour itself is deduced;
*(db-backup)* = the DB side comes from the private-server backups, not the 2016 build. None of these rows was observed
in a capture unless marked *(captured)*; `superseded: *(captured)* …` = a capture showed that the bug as stated does not
exist (L00-B6). *Owner*: the item whose document describes it.

### 6.2 Entries from free-text notes and client-side mismatches

Hand-written by X01 (part 2). Sources: the free text of the items written before the `(original bug)` convention
(L00, W01, Z01–Z03, C01, C02, P01, P03; grep for `bug`, `sic`, `never reach`, `uninitialised`, `ignored`,
`copy-paste`, `typo`), `(sic)` notes of later items that no bug row covers, C01 §5 (server → client opcodes no 2016
client dispatcher compares, re-checked against `re-data/client-handlers.json` and the owning documents), and the client
texts of `docs/protocol/client-error-codes.json`. Everything the pre-convention items described in free text already
has a row in their bug tables (§6.1), except the entries below. P02's bugs (P02-B1 … B40) now sit in §6.1.11 (the generator reads
P02's table in `docs/protocol/capture-validation.md` §12, found by its header, not by number; §8 when X01 ran). Error *codes* a server sends that the client shows no text
for are recorded per handler by the owning items (their rows say "no client text") and are not repeated here.

#### 6.2.1 Free-text notes and `(sic)` notes

| ID | Handler (§ in the owning doc) | 2016 behaviour | Effect | Likely intent | Evidence | Owner |

0x580A (C01-B4): the GameFrameWork dispatcher FUN_004be490 compares it and ignores it ("accepted and ignored"). The
client-facing ones already in §6.1: 0x0830 / 0x0C24 / 0x4026 (Z01-B5), 0x1C16 (Z11-B23), 0x2036 / 0x2038 / 0x203A /
0x203B (Z02-B5), 0x2045 (Z02-B6), 0x3032 (Z04-B12), 0x8C57 (Z05-B12, never sent), 0x980A (W03-B20), 0x982B (W03-B21).
Likely intent in every row: client support for the packet, or drop the send *(inferred)*; the rows say which matters.

## 7. Remaining gaps

What the specification does not settle yet, as of the X01 sweep, updated by X03 (§7.1, §7.3) and X04 (§7.1, §7.2, §7.3 P02 row). `docs/plan/progress.md` (generated by
`coverage.py --overview`) shows every registered handler of the seven servers cited by some document (1581 / 1581)
and every function up to three calls below them (2508 / 2508; calls through vtables are not counted, so this is a lower
bound of the code read). The gaps are therefore not unread handlers but unsettled details: evidence that only a capture,
a lost source or the client can give, and the open points each item listed. §7.1 names the open work items, §7.2 the
gaps that cut across items, §7.3 each document's own list, §7.4 what the X01 sweep settled or added.

### 7.1 Open work items

| [P05](../plan/items/P05.md) | not started | the protocol README's flows against capture batch 4 (back to character select `0x0C18 {1}`, the quit's link traffic, the client's own logout after losing the zone socket, the WM's `0x1002 {0x145}` with a zone down); README §3.12 here already carries them *(captured)* |

### 7.2 Gaps that cut across items

- **Captures.** Only the session flows are *(captured)*: login, token login, world list and select, world login,
  character select and load, map login, login complete, map change through a gate and a town portal, logout to the
  world list, the duplicate-login kick and the heartbeats (`docs/protocol/capture-validation.md`; §3.3–§3.12 here mark
  them). Every gameplay feature (combat, items and ITEMDB, trade and booths, party, raid, guild, academy, tournament,
  quests, KQ, instances, mini-house, wedding, GM commands, the DB answers) is read in code only, and so are the effects
  of almost all bugs in §6 (`code; effect *(inferred)*`). X02 turned them into scenarios 14–44 with predicted packets
  (`docs/plan/capture-scenarios.md`); X05 re-rated them after batch 3, X07 does so after batch 4. Since X01, P02's
  batches 2–4 captured some of them too (the server links, items I–III, combat and death, sight and GM visibility,
  create / rename refusals, session loss and zone kills, skills, stat points, reset items, abnormal states, revive by
  skill: §3.14, §3.31, §3.32 and the *(captured)* rows of §3.12–§3.13); party, raid, guild, trade, booths, quests, KQ,
  instances and most failure paths are still read in code only (capture-validation §14, §15).
- **Lost DB sources.** The procedures listed in D01 §7, D02 §16, D03 §16 and D05 §9 have no body in any backup; D06 §2–§6 gives the
  narrowest contract the binds force, with tables and columns *(ours)* (D06 §11, §9.6). The schema is the 2019 restore
  (D06 §9); the w00 bodies carry 2017–2019 private fixes, and D06 §8 records which version a rebuild should follow.
  Error codes that only these procedures produce (D01 §7, D02 §16, D04 §12, D07 §4) stay *(inferred)* or unknown.
- **No PDB for Character and GameLog.** Their names and offsets are ours or matched by code shape (S01 §8: the
  `CSQLP*` parameter structs come from the Account build; D02–D05 *(inferred)* marks). The D02 / Z28 disagreement on the
  `CardInform` record order (Z28 §7) is one consequence and stays open.
- **Zone start-up script.** The order of the `zs_start_*` / `zs_service_*` stages comes from the certification
  server; only the private-server replacement exists (Z10 §10). Z10 §7.2 lists the data dependencies any order must keep.
- **Floating-point formulas.** Hit rate, damage, critical, experience and the stat layers are read from x87 code
  (Z03 §11, Z12 N., Z22 §13, Z29 §4): operand order is certain, the CRT precision and rounding were not run.
- **Client-side behaviour.** Each client-facing packet names its client handler, but several UI consequences were not
  followed: how the client obtains `EncHandle` for `0x581A` (Z08 §15, Z16 N.), what it does with `0x7C07` after leaving
  character select (W04 §19), client-side scenarios (Z15 §9), the cast bar after a silent silver-wing failure (Z18 §8,
  Z26 §8), Volley's lone `0x244E` (Z25 §7), the dialog after the last SAY (Z15-B12). Codes without a client text are
  recorded per handler (§6.2 intro).
- **2017-only facts.** Field and opcode names marked *(pdb2017)* that no 2016 binary confirms (about 10 % of the 2017
  names conflict with 2016 readings, AGENTS.md); C02 and S01 list the known conflicts.
- **Latent paths of the 2016 data.** Many condition, effect and quest kinds have no row in the shipped SHN tables, so
  their code was read but never exercised (Z15 §9, Z17 §5, Z20 §8, Z21 §6, Z24 §9, Z28 §7). A data set from another
  region could reach them, and with them the latent bugs those items list.

### 7.4 Settled or added by the X01 sweep

- **KQ joiners on a zone without map 0** (part 6 gap): settled. `0x580F` FUN_00499240 fills an entrance slot
  (`KingdomQuestEntrance::operator[]` FUN_00496150) with the joiners on **every** zone, outside the map-0 test; the gate
  keeper `erb_happen` FUN_00496280 searches that slot, not the KQ player list, so each zone links its own joiners to
  map 0 (§3.23 step 6, rewritten). Z16 §2.2 is right; part 6 had read the player list for the search.
- **Failed link reserve** (part 5 gap): `0x180E` FUN_0049b510 with HP 0 or mode 3 undoes nothing and tells nobody:
  X01-B5 (§6.2.1, effect *(inferred)*; a capture would need a death in the WM round trip).
- **S01 size flags** (S01 §2.3, §8): all settled now. Zone 0x3034 (4 of 6 bytes, `Error` off the wire) and 0x8C4C
  (12 bytes, 6 stale) are Z13 §3.3 / §3.1; 0x3065 is Z05-B11; AccountLog 0x0C2B / 0x0C3C are D01-B2; 0x380A is the
  `ref` alone and 0x7C07 the documented short form (W04); 0x9868 is an extractor artefact (S01). WM **0x9407** with 22
  bytes is `fc_NC_HOLY_PROMISE_DEL_DOWN_REQ` sending a DEL_DOWN_ACK body under the DEL_UP_ACK opcode: X01-B6, and W04
  §12.3's "0x940B" is superseded (§5.3). WM **0x1110** with 14 bytes is a layout-extractor false positive: FUN_0040eff0
  (`fc_NC_GUILD_TOURNAMENT_DB_GET_ACK`, W03) and `CGuildTournament::ApplyResult` FUN_0045afd0 fill byte tables whose bytes 1–2 read as 0x1110; the tutorial
  question is sent empty (W01 §4.2). Zone 0x340D's attribute tail past the 2017 struct is read as such in D03 §2.1.
- **OPTool connection list** `0x2802`: D01 §2's entry order is superseded (§5.7; `packets-account.md` already shows the 2016 PDB layout, which agrees); the time field's top two bits are never written: X01-B7 (review).
- **Still open from X01's own reading:** X01-B3 (logout save against a quick re-select) rests on the Character server
  not ordering requests of different links (D02's worker model; not proven); X01-B4 / X01-B5 effects are *(inferred)*.

## 8. Not reconstructed / uncertain

About this file itself (the open points of the specification are §7):

- **An index, not a re-reading.** §2–§4 summarise the items' documents; X01 re-read the 2016 code only where it says so:
  W01-B5 (`GetClientValidation` FUN_0040a200), the save paths (`ws_save2worldmanager` FUN_004536f0, `ws_save2DB`
  FUN_00455a10, `so_Unmark` FUN_00558900, `zs_disconnect` FUN_005abb60), X01-B1…B7, `0x2802` (FUN_00404dd0) and the KQ
  entrance slot (FUN_00499240). Everything else carries the owning item's evidence, and a step that item marked
  *(inferred)* stays inferred here even where the marker is not repeated in a sequence table.
- **Message order in §3.** Hops on one link keep their order; the order between different links (for example the
  Character server's answer against the WM's next packet) is what the code sends first, not a guarantee, unless the step
  is *(captured)*. X01-B3 is the one place where this matters and is itself *(inferred)*.
- **Superseded statements (§5).** Collected from the headings named in §5 and the passages found by grep (`Correction`,
  "Answers to", "For <item>", corrections inside pseudocode text). A correction written in running text under another
  heading can still be missing; X02's `git log` pass over the docs is the cross-check.
- **Register (§6.1).** Generated from table rows; the *Evidence* column is a heuristic over the row's words (§6
  intro), so an inferred effect described without the word "inferred" shows as `code`. The owning document's text wins.
  Rows the owners did not tag in place (83 IDs) are still listed.
- **Older open-point lists (§7.3)** were shortened, not re-checked point by point against the later items; the third
  column says where to look first.
- **Glossary (§4.7)** maps only the older names used at least five times; `docs/knowledge/S01-renames.tsv` has the rest.
- **The X03 update (2026-11-16) copies, it does not re-read.** The rows X03 added to §5 restate `docs/knowledge/X02.md`
  §0, Z31 §0, Z32 "Corrections to done items", W06 §0 and D08 §0 in this file's row format; the evidence is the source
  row's (its re-check ID in `docs/review/confidence.md` §3). A script matched each of the 69 X02 §0 rows one-to-one to
  a §5 row by its `FUN_` addresses, opcodes, backticked names and re-check IDs (sweep, part 6); the paragraph "`wm-pdb` member names" after that table (W14) was added in the review (part 7). Where an older X01 row
  and an X02 row disagree, the X02 row (later, re-checked) wins; X03 did not delete the X01 rows.
- **Register sources since X01.** §6.1 is now written by `docs/plan/status/X03-register.py` (wraps X01's generator):
  X02-B1 … B12 come from `confidence.md` §5 with the reachability of `knowledge/X02.md` §3 as *Evidence*; D08-B1 … B12
  have their own group (§6.1.8). The §5.9 marks are X03's reading of which X02 / follow-up rows change an entry;
  L00-B5 is not marked (X02 G5 renames the constant, the register row states no size).
- **Register since X04.** `docs/plan/status/X04-register.py` runs X03's wrapper and post-processes its rows: the §5.9
  marks of P02's batches 2–3 (L00-B6, L01-B11, Z04-B6); the *(captured)* §s of an ID that capture-validation §12 lists
  twice joined (D03-B11 §7.6.1 + §19.3 / §19.4, Z09-B72 §17.4 + §20.4; X01's reader kept only the last); the Evidence
  of P02-B1 … B32; and cross notes (P02-B10 / B11 = W05-B15; P02-B29 ↔ W04-B54 and the un-numbered W04 §2.1 whisper
  flag; P02-B30 ↔ Z11-B8, Z18-B13, Z06-B7; P02-B16 ↔ P02-B32; P02-B15 ↔ D03-B11). W05-B15 gets *(captured)* through
  P02-B10 / B11.
- **Register since X06.** `docs/plan/status/X06-register.py` runs X04's wrapper and post-processes its rows for P02's
  batch 4: the Evidence of P02-B33 … B40 (capture-validation §22–§25; X04's table stops at P02-B32); §25.9 joined into
  D04-B13 (X04's reader skips the second-table row "P02-B36 / D04-B13", two IDs in one cell); Z07-B2 qualified (the
  captured `nLockIndex` is unwritten but happened to be 0); the §5.9 mark of W05-B25; cross notes P02-B35 ↔ W05-B25 and
  P02-B37 ↔ D04-B5.
- **The X04 update (2026-11-16) copies P02, it does not re-read code.** Its §5 rows restate capture-validation §13
  (status "→ X01" / "→ X04") with the `@ FUN_…` of `docs/knowledge/P02.md` "Corrections"; its §3 rows restate §11; its
  register evidence is §12. Every §13 row of batches 1–3 is either in §5 or listed with its reason in
  `docs/knowledge/X04.md` §2. Where an older X01 / X03 row and an X04 row disagree, the X04 row (captured) wins.
- **The X06 update (2026-11-16) copies P02 batch 4, it does not re-read code.** Its §5 rows restate the 13 §13 rows
  "→ X06" with the `@ FUN_…` of `docs/knowledge/P02.md` "Corrections" (batch 4); its §3 marks restate §11's batch-4 rows
  and §22–§25; its register evidence is §12. Handler addresses in the new §3 rows come from `handlers.json`, not from
  reading the functions. Where an older row and an X06 row disagree (the quit's four saves, the Kickout timer, the
  auto-save interval), the X06 row (captured) wins; the older rows stay, superseded by §5.
- **`docs/plan/progress.md` item marks.** `coverage.py --overview` counts an item as done when any of its status
  file's first eight lines contains the word "done", so an item in progress whose State line reads
  `part <n> of <m> done` shows ✓ (X03 itself while parts 6–7 ran). §7.1 reads the State line instead.

## 9. Coverage boundary

X01 has no handler scope (`coverage.py X01`: "X01 has no scope table; coverage does not apply"); `coverage.py --overview`
shows no uncovered handler or function in any server (§7). What X01 found and did not settle itself:

| Found | Covered by | Argument |
|---|---|---|
| Protocol README statements contradicted by the captures (seed on accept, server heartbeat, captured flows) | P04 (open; plan "README §1.4, §1.5, §2 and the Unknowns list agree with capture-validation §2 / §7"; that §7 is today's §11) | P04 owns `docs/protocol/README.md`, which X01 may not edit; §5.1 here warns readers until then |
| Every claim read in code only (gameplay features, bug effects marked *(inferred)*, the client-side points of §7.2) | X02 (done since; X03: its output `docs/review/confidence.md` / `docs/plan/capture-scenarios.md` covers this; plan: "for every claim still medium or low after the re-check, write a scenario … the prediction") | settling them needs captures, DB setups or targeted re-reads with an adversarial reviewer, which is X02's method; X01 indexes them (§7.2, §7.3) |
| The per-document open points of §7.3 | the owning items' documents (each row's §) and X02 (same plan line) | the owners recorded them as open on purpose; none is an unread handler or function (progress 100 %); points that need evidence go to X02's scenarios |
| Procedures without source, schema of 2016 (§7.2) | D06 §11 (done; documents the contracts and says what stays open) | no further source exists in the repository; only a 2016 database dump would settle them |
| Zone start-up stage order from the certification server | Z10 §7, §10 (done; documents the dependencies any order must keep) | the 2016 answer is not in the repository |
| D02 / Z28 `CardInform` record order | Z28 §7 (done; documents both readings) | the Character binary has no PDB; one capture of a card album (X02 scenario) settles it |
| `CGuildTournament::ApplyResult` FUN_0045afd0, FUN_0040eff0 (cited here only for the 0x1110 extractor artefact) | W03 (done; FUN_0040eff0 = `fc_NC_GUILD_TOURNAMENT_DB_GET_ACK` and the result path are documented there) | X01 only checked that they do not send 0x1110 |
| 2015 binaries | none (AGENTS.md: "Only for diffing; do not document them") | out of scope for the whole reconstruction |

No follow-up item was created: everything X01 found either fits P04 or X02 (both open when X01 ran; X02 is done since, X03) or is documented as open by a
done item whose document already describes it.
